# Claim Ledger

This is the canonical claim table for the L16/Lumen reverse-engineering effort.

## Rules

- This file is the authority for claim status.
- Other canonical docs may summarize or organize claims, but may not silently strengthen them.
- `PARTIAL` claims may not be written as closed prose in downstream docs.
- `0 hits` claims must keep their tested scope.
- `SPEC_READY` means safe to write into the clean-room parity spec.

## Status Vocabulary

### Claim Status

- `PROVEN`
- `PARTIAL`
- `OPEN`
- `REFUTED`
- `SUPERSEDED`

### Per-Zoom Validation

- `VERIFIED`
- `VERIFIED_SAME_MECHANISM`
- `PARTIAL`
- `OPEN`
- `REFUTED`
- `N/A`

### Implementation Readiness

- `SPEC_READY`
- `BLOCKER`
- `REFERENCE_ONLY`

| `CLM-MERGE-005` | IRAMP Partner Gate / Record Population / Refined Tuple | The local IRAMP block-matching path is guarded by partner-record-vector emptiness: `0x3692dc` compares begin/end, `0x3692e4` jumps to `0x369f2a` when empty, non-empty falls through toward first SAD at `0x3694b1`, and partner records are `0x280` bytes. Runtime proof observes non-empty gate and first SAD at all four canonical zooms; empty-gate runtime hits are observed at `28mm` and `70mm` only so far. Static plus runtime proof bounds the first-hit partner-record append/population path across all four canonical zooms: the path reaches `0x368b02`, and the physical `0x280` record layout is four int32 scalar fields followed by thirteen contiguous `0x30` descriptor-like blocks. Static plus runtime proof also bounds the live non-empty consumer path across all four canonical zooms through coarse SIMD SAD / `phminposuw` winner selection, local absolute-difference refinement, guarded float refinement, 16x16 bilinear vec4 resampling, `0x36cde0`, and a three-float scratch write at `0x369e7e..0x369e91`. Follow-up static plus runtime proof narrows `0x36cde0`: it consumes the two prepared 16x16 `vec4` patches, computes patch-statistics / fixed-transform / weighted-reduction work, returns `sqrt(xmm0 * xmm1)`, and the caller stores that live `xmm0` scalar as the tuple's third float. Follow-up static plus runtime proof bounds `0x36e530` as the immediate accumulator-prep call: it receives `rbp-0x4240`, performs reciprocal/selector normalization plus fixed SIMD transform/reduction work, returns `scratch+0x1580` in `rax`, and the accumulator consumes that source block with a 16-by-16 outer product of captured scalar weights. Follow-up static plus runtime proof bounds the first downstream tuple consumer: it reads the third tuple scalar at `0x36a7d8`, reads the first two tuple floats at `0x36a803` / `0x36a814`, passes an adjusted coordinate pair to `0x372a00`, forms multiplier `(t + 2 * max(0, t - 0.5), t, t, t)`, reaches multiply-add loop `0x36a8c0..0x36a8cb` across the canonical four-zoom quartet, and adds the third scalar into a running scalar sum initialized from `0x5df904 = 0.200000003`. Follow-up static plus runtime proof bounds the immediate post-reciprocal weighted-add path: `0x19e7d0` copies/scales descriptor-backed `vec4` buffers by the reciprocal vector, and `0x36aa30..0x36aa57` blends `reciprocal * 0.2` into lane 3, applies `weight[inner] * weight[outer]`, adds into the destination `vec4`, and is reached across the canonical four-zoom quartet. Follow-up static plus runtime proof bounds the immediate post-weighted-add shaping path: `0x36abf0..0x36ac15` applies a lane-3-weighted clamped vector update with first-hit scale `(2,0,0,0)` and clamp bounds `[-0.1,0.1]`, and `0x36ad50..0x36adac` applies a fixed 3-vector transform with lane 3 forced to `1.0`; both sites are reached across the canonical four-zoom quartet. Follow-up static plus runtime proof bounds the caller-side handoff after IRAMP returns: the caller validates the returned descriptor dimensions against ROI, wraps the `rbp-0x60` descriptor at `rbp-0x88`, calls helper `0xd76a0`, and static helper inspection shows source `vec4` lanes are squared into the destination descriptor; the handoff is reached across the canonical four-zoom quartet. Follow-up static plus runtime proof bounds the caller-side vector-scale handoff after square-copy: the caller builds a wrapper over the `rbp-0x70` descriptor with a vector at wrapper `+0x10`, calls helper `0x2d7320`, and static helper inspection shows source `vec4` lanes are multiplied by that vector into the destination descriptor; the handoff is reached across the canonical four-zoom quartet. Follow-up static plus runtime proof bounds the caller-side `0x3e5720` executor setup after vector-scale: it allocates/resizes a 6-byte-element destination descriptor, builds callback vtable `0x66b020`, dispatches generic executor `0x5670`, and its visible worker maps source 16-byte `vec4` rows to destination 6-byte rows before calling row callback `0x38a30`; the setup is reached across the canonical four-zoom quartet. Follow-up static plus runtime proof bounds row callback `0x38a30`: for the observed `512`-wide rows it repacks source `vec4` lanes 0..2 into float triples, calls `0xbfef0` with `ecx = 0` and count `3 * width`, and the used `0xbfef0` branch converts float channels to 16-bit binary16 bit patterns; first captured callback rows match the static conversion formula across the canonical four-zoom quartet. | `CLM-DEMOSAIC-002` | Demosaic Formula | `DemosaickLightV1` is an exact four-phase residual-interpolation demosaic. CFA gains and a phase-preserving source-only endpoint extension produce `S`. A 21-tap green-site `P` guide, a first four-direction correction at red/blue sites (`H=P` at green), and a refined four-direction correction at green sites (`A=H` at red/blue) produce `A`; derived `P/H/A` rows are evaluated through finite virtual halos. `B=S-A` uses virtual vertical rows and asymmetric horizontal guards. Inverse-gradient interpolation of channel-matched residuals plus `A` produces RGBA float32 with alpha `1`. | `CLM-C6-001` | Camera Routing | On the tested canonical tele bridge-HDR path, C6/key `15` fires and is constructed with public `CapturedImage.is_enabled = 1`, then `libcp+0x3c90a5` clears that byte to `0`. - `CLM-PIPELINE-001` five-band payload-type addendum (clean-room, Phoenix side):
  A `L16_Phoenix` re-derivation of the installed `lt::Internal::Pipeline` object layout, performed independently of the canonical bundles and without rendering, scoring, or consulting any parity frame, reproduces the fixed order table exactly and adds object-level structure it did not state. The band arithmetic is `band k base = 0x60 + 0x420*k` for `k = 0..4`, `slot j = base + 0x40*j` for `j = 0..15`, run vector at `base + 0x400`, and per-slot fields `+0x00..+0x1F` `std::function` SOO buffer, `+0x20` `__f_`, `+0x30` support/halo `int`, `+0x34` alignment modulus `int`, `+0x38` scale `float`. There are **five** bands, not three, and each is a distinct payload type named from Itanium-ABI `type_info` reached through the `__func` vtable address point: band 0 `BayerFloatPipelinePayload`, band 1 `BayerPipelinePayload`, band 2 a second Bayer-family band, band 3 `ColorPipelinePayload`, band 4 `MonoPipelinePayload`. Codex's three named loops map on as `0x33f180` shared by bands 1 and 2 (Bayer), `0x33f480` band 0 (BayerFloat), `0x33fb30` band 3 (Color); the fifth runner `0x3403f0` (Mono) is outside the parent bundle's scope. Five thin non-virtual wrappers select a band by direct call — `0x31acf0`→0, `0x31af30`→1, `0x31b110`→3, `0x31b470`→2 then 4, `0x318290`→1 — so there is no runtime band selector. Sixteen `set*` bodies each write **the same slot index in all five bands**; that five-fold repetition at constant stride is what over-determines the layout. Reading the constructor tail `0x32a84c..0x32aa7b` confirms exactly two constructor-default installs per band, at slots 2 and 11, which land precisely on the parent bundle's two constructor defaults at vector indices 3 and 11 — an independent reconciliation of the permutation from the opposite direction. No claim status changes; this is object-layout and payload-type structure beneath an already `PROVEN` order.
- `CLM-PIPELINE-001` / `CLM-CORRECTION-001` HighlightRestore band-incidence addendum:
  `Pipeline::setHighlightRestore` (`0x331410`) is a two-state switch stored at `Pipeline+0x1514` — `0` tears the slots down, `1` installs, any other value throws — reached from exactly one call site, `0x3189eb`, with the enum taken from a configuration record (`esi = dword [cfg+0x38]`, `cfg` looked up by `0x3239d0` from the settings key at `0x6714d0`). One call installs slot 8 of **all five** bands with a different functor each: band 0 vtable `0x668b10` / `operator()` `0x34def0` / descriptor `{1,1}` wrapping raw function pointer `0x32b770`; band 1 `$_33` vtable `0x65bf18` / `0x343e10` / `{support 9, align 2}`; band 2 cloned from band 1 through the out-of-line `std::function::swap` at `0x34c430`; band 3 `$_34` vtable `0x65bf98` / `0x3440b0` / `{support 3, align 1}`; band 4 vtable `0x668c38` / `0x34e090` / `{1,1}` wrapping `0x32d390`. Slot 8 is **vector index 2**, so HighlightRestore executes third — after the two hot-pixel stages and before Bayer normalization/materialization, cross-talk and demosaic — making it an early raw-Bayer stage rather than a post-demosaic stage. Joining this to the capped four-focal census in [lldb_src1_virtual_target_census_four_zoom.md](/Volumes/Dev/L16_Lumen_ReverseEngineering/docs/evidence/lldb_src1_virtual_target_census_four_zoom.md): the Bayer loop observes `0x65bf18 -> 0x343e10` at `28/35/70/150mm`, while the Color loop's six observed families at `28mm`/`35mm` exclude `0x3440b0` and the BayerFloat loop's families exclude `0x34def0`. The HighlightRestore live on the canonical profile-3 bridge-HDR path is therefore the **Bayer-domain `$_33`**, not the `Image<vec4x32f>` Color variant. Because that census caps each site at `512` hits and is explicitly non-exhaustive, the absence is strong negative incidence and not an unreachability proof; why one setter call populates band 3 slot 8 without it ever firing is not closed, and no editor-path (`CLM-COMPAT-001`) incidence for `$_34` is claimed either way. Both parent claims remain `PROVEN` / `SPEC_READY`. Clean-room consequence recorded in `L16_Phoenix/phoenix/PARITY_BLOCKER_MAP.md` Class D: the Phoenix Stage 9b kernel transcribed from `0x3440b0` has no demonstrated incidence and must not be wired in as recovered behaviour until `0x343e10` is transcribed.
- `CLM-PIPELINE-001` / `CLM-CORRECTION-001` HighlightRestore Bayer-kernel transcription addendum (clean-room, Phoenix side):
  The precondition set by the preceding addendum is now discharged: `0x343e10` has been followed to its arithmetic core and that core is transcribed instruction-for-instruction, with **no parity frame rendered, scored, or consulted at any point**. The chain is `$_33::operator()` `0x343e10` -> `lt::RestoreHighlightsBayer` outer `0x30b770` -> one of four CFA-phase kernels `0x30b9f0` / `0x30dcc0` / `0x30ff60` / `0x3121f0`, selected by `lt::Vec2<int> bayerMode`. Kernel `0x30b9f0` inner loop A (`0x30be60..0x30cce1`) is fully transcribed in `L16_Phoenix/_parity_run/STAGE9B_HRB_KERNEL_MATH.md` §3; inner loop B (`0x30cda0..0x30dc70`) is fully transcribed there in §7 (added 2026-08-04), closing `GAP-9B-3d.5`.
  Entry ABI at `0x30b9f0`: `xmm0 = a` (black point), `xmm1 = b` (white point), `rdi = dst`, `rsi = line buffer A`, `rdx = line buffer B`, `ecx = tile width`, `r8d = tile height`. Buffer A is a 16-row ring with a 4-pixel left halo pulling from the source (`A::row` = `0x314600`); buffer B is a 1-row lazy buffer pulling from A (`B::row` = `0x314d50`). The outer loop steps `y += 2` and pins ten source rows per iteration, `y-4 .. y+5`, which **independently reproduces the `{support 9, align 2}` slot-8 descriptor** recorded in the band-incidence addendum from the opposite direction — the descriptor was read from the installer, the halo from the kernel body, and they agree.
  The algorithm is gradient-directed **Hamilton-Adams** green interpolation feeding a chroma-guided highlight reconstruction. Per output pair: a gate `if (B::row(y)[x] < (uint16)(int)(0.985f*b)) { dst = src; continue; }` short-circuits non-highlight pixels; the centre green is estimated scalar with Q10 white-balance ratios (`Q = (int)(1024.0f * v.y/v.x)` on the centre-channel Laplacian, floor `(int)(-0.04f*b)`, direction chosen by `gh = |hL| + |right-left|` versus `gv = |vL| + |below-above|`); four diagonal greens are estimated in one SSE lane-quad at `(y-1,x-1)`, `(y-1,x+1)`, `(y+1,x-1)`, `(y+1,x+1)` with `Q = (int)(1024.0f * v.y/v.z)`; `H` and `V` are `4*green`, which is why both paths finish with `*0.25f`. The diagonal estimate is combined by inverse-distance chroma weights `w_i = rcpps(|G_i - g0| / (b-a) + 0.009765625f)` over differences `D_i = src_i*(v.y/v.z) - G_i`, giving `Z = (g0 + (sum w_i D_i) * rcpss(sum w_i)) * (v.z/v.y)`. The restore then normalizes `t = (c - a)/((b-a)*v)`, forms a per-channel saturation ramp `s = clamp((t - 0.85f/v) * (6.6666665f*v), 0, 1)` (i.e. `clamp(((c-a)/(b-a) - 0.85)/0.15, 0, 1)`), redistributes unclipped energy `M = U * rcpss(3.0000100135803223f - S)` with `S = s.r+s.g+s.b`, blends `res = t + min(S,1) * max(t' - t, 0)`, applies a desaturation pull toward the mean along a fixed normalized axis gated by `C1 = min(max(S-1,0),1)` and a direction dot product using `rsqrtps`, and finally a max-channel pull gated by `C2 = max(S-2.0f, 0.0f)` **with no upper clamp**. Output is `cvttss2si(res.lane * (b-a)*v_channel + a)` stored 16-bit; site 2 emits **lane 1 of the same restore vector** via `movshdup`, so one restore computation serves both pixels of the pair. Every literal is pinned to an instruction and read back out of the image: `0.985f` at `0x5f3e24`, `-0.04f` at `0x5f3e1c`, `0.85f` at `0x5f3e20`, `6.6666665f` at `0x5e7420`, `0.009765625f` (= `10/1024`) at `0x5f3e30`, `3.0000100135803223f` at `0x5f3e28`, `-0.9f` at `0x5f3e40`, `0.9f` at `0x5c3534`, `0.33333334f` at `0x5aae88`, `-2.0f` at `0x5a8874`, `1024.0f` at `0x5e3180`, `0.25f` at `0x5a8190`. This closes `GAP-9B-3d.1`.
  The four phase kernels are proved to be **one algorithm under four index assignments**, from the binary alone. Pairwise `difflib` similarity over the mnemonic streams is `0.9855` for `{0x30b9f0, 0x3121f0}` and `0.9883` for `{0x30dcc0, 0x30ff60}`, with the two cross-block pairs near `0.50`; the low cross-block figures are an **alignment artifact**, not a difference — in the G-first kernels the two inner loops exchange roles, so the sequence aligner matches loop A of one against loop B of the other. A permutation-invariant mnemonic-multiset comparison settles it: against `0x30b9f0` the deltas are `0x30dcc0` = 4 instructions, `0x30ff60` = 6, `0x3121f0` = 6, out of 2061. Reading the four kernels' output gain slots against their preamble writes yields the phase map `0x30b9f0 -> RGGB`, `0x30dcc0 -> GRBG`, `0x30ff60 -> GBRG`, `0x3121f0 -> BGGR`, and therefore the project-wide fact that **`lt::Vec2<int> bayerMode` is the `(column, row)` offset of the red site within the 2x2 cell**: `(0,0) = RGGB`, `(1,0) = GRBG`, `(0,1) = GBRG`, `(1,1) = BGGR`. Any Phoenix code asserting a CFA phase convention must be checked against this. This closes `GAP-9B-3d.2`. `GAP-9B-3b` also closes: the gain vector `v` is `payload[0]`, a shared `Vec3<float>*`.
  Three bit-parity hazards are recorded so a naive port cannot silently miss them. (1) `rcpps` / `rcpss` / `rsqrtps` are used **with no Newton-Raphson refinement**, so `1.0f/x` and `1.0f/sqrtf(x)` will not reproduce the result; the 12-bit hardware approximation must be emulated exactly, for which the existing [bundle_runtime_x86_rcpss_rcpps_exact_emulation.md](/Users/ryaker/Dev/L16_Lumen_ReverseEngineering/docs/evidence/bundle_runtime_x86_rcpss_rcpps_exact_emulation.md) addendum already supplies an integer-only bit formula verified over 6,242,316 cases (`GAP-9B-3d.6`). (2) The horizontal sums are associated pairwise, not left-to-right, by `shufpd 0x1 -> addps -> shufps 0xb1 -> addps`, and float addition is not associative. `GAP-9B-3d.7` is now **closed** by addendum III below, which gives the exact operand order `fadd(fadd(v1,v3), fadd(v0,v2))` — the outer add takes the odd-lane pair as its left operand. (3) Both 16-bit narrowings are **truncating and non-saturating** — the scalar green via a 32-bit sign test `cmovns` feeding a 16-bit store, and the output via `cvttss2si` feeding a 16-bit store.
  Still open and explicitly not claimed: what statistic buffer B carries (`0x314d50`; the `B < 0.985*b` gate suggests a local neighbourhood maximum, unproved — `GAP-9B-3d.3`); the identity of the object at `ctx + 0x198` that supplies `a` and `b` (`GAP-9B-3d.4`); which setter populates slot 8 in the other four bands (`GAP-9B-3c`); and whether `$_34` / `lt::RestoreHighlights` is reachable on any profile (`GAP-9B-3f`). No claim status changes; this is recovered kernel arithmetic beneath an already `PROVEN` order and an already-recorded band incidence. The Class D consequence in `L16_Phoenix/phoenix/PARITY_BLOCKER_MAP.md` is now dischargeable for the Bayer variant only: the `0x3440b0` vec4 kernel still has no demonstrated incidence and still must not be wired into `phoenix_fuse` as recovered behaviour.


- `CLM-PIPELINE-001` / `CLM-CORRECTION-001` HighlightRestore Bayer-kernel addendum II — inner loop B (clean-room, Phoenix side, 2026-08-04):

  `GAP-9B-3d.5` is **closed**. Inner loop B of phase kernel `0x30b9f0` (`0x30cda0..0x30dc70`) is transcribed instruction-for-instruction in `L16_Phoenix/_parity_run/STAGE9B_HRB_KERNEL_MATH.md` §7. Both inner loops of the kernel are now recovered. **No parity frame was rendered, scored, or consulted for any statement in §7** — every claim is an instruction read out of `dis_HRB_phase_30b9f0.txt` or a float read out of `libcp.dylib` at a named virtual address.

  One recorded expectation is **corrected**: loop B is not loop A with the row aliases shifted and the `v.x`/`v.z` roles swapped. Loop B's site 1 is a **green** centre — the centre green is the sample itself, and the four interpolated greens are at the *cardinal* neighbours `(y+1,x±1)` (blue sites) and `(y,x)`, `(y+2,x)` (red sites), with the horizontal pair yielding the blue estimate and the vertical pair the red. That is a different interpolation case from anything in §3, and it is why loop B carries two independent gates and two independent restore computations where loop A carries one of each. Only loop B's site 2 is the structural mirror of loop A's site 1.

  The strongest new result is that the highlight-restore cascade is **one 96-instruction body instantiated four times**, at `0x30c3a2`, `0x30cb7b`, `0x30d49c` and `0x30db0b`. This is established by comparing the four windows with register names erased and rip-relative operands resolved to absolute virtual addresses: instructions 5..100 are identical in all four, with two apparent exceptions that both resolve — `mulps X, xmm15` versus `mulps X, [rbp-0x180]` (`0x30bdd0: movaps xmm15, [rbp-0x180]`), and `pxor` versus `xorps` (both zero the register). The `+ a` operand likewise differs only as `[rbp-0x170]` versus `xmm14`, with `0x30ccfb: movaps xmm14, [rbp-0x170]`. Consequently the arithmetic already recorded in §3.6 is the whole of the arithmetic for every site in both loops; only the per-site vector assembly and the per-site output tail differ.

  The `bayerMode = (0,0)` → RGGB map is now confirmed from a second, independent direction. The loop-invariant preamble computes `[rbp-0x250] = (b-a)·v.x`, `[rbp-0x1b0] = (b-a)·v.y` and `[rbp-0x24c] = (b-a)·v.z` at `0x30bc04`, `0x30bc45` and `0x30bc52`, and the four output tails consume them in the site order A1, A2, B1, B2 as `X Y Y Z`. The same block also pins `[rbp-0x180]`, `[rbp-0xd0]`, `[rbp-0xe0]`, `[rbp-0x100]`, `[rbp-0x104]`, `[rbp-0x13c]`, `[rbp-0x140]`, `[rbp-0x1d0]`, `[rbp-0x1e0]`, `[rbp-0x134]` and `[rbp-0x138]`, which makes the saturation ramp `s = clamp(((c-a)/(b-a) - 0.85)/0.15, 0, 1)` algebraically exact rather than a fit.

  Four parity hazards are newly recorded, all static and all reproducible without a parity frame. First, **loop B truncates its neighbour greens and loop A does not**: loop B computes `float((uint16)(int)(0.25f * (float)(uint16)H))` while loop A's SIMD path scales by `0.25f` and keeps the float — the same mathematical quantity, quantised differently per loop. Second, the Hamilton–Adams gradient magnitude is `|v| + (v < 0)`, not `|v|`, because the `sar 31 / xor / shr 31 / add` idiom adds one for negative inputs. Third, `cmovg dx, ax` and `cmovs dx, ax` are 16-bit conditional moves whose conditions are decided on the 32-bit value, so a clamping C translation diverges. Fourth, the output channel is chosen by lane and by a different instruction per lane: lane 0 direct for red, `movshdup` for green, `shufpd 0x1` for blue.

  Still open and explicitly not claimed, unchanged by this addendum: `GAP-9B-3d.3` (what statistic buffer B carries), `GAP-9B-3d.4` (the object at `ctx + 0x198`), `GAP-9B-3d.6` (exact unrefined `rcpss`/`rcpps`/`rsqrtps` emulation — parity-critical; reuse `bundle_runtime_x86_rcpss_rcpps_exact_emulation.md`, do not re-derive), `GAP-9B-3c`, and `GAP-9B-3f`. (`GAP-9B-3d.7`, listed as open when this addendum was written, is closed by addendum III below.) No claim status changes. The `0x3440b0` vec4 kernel still has no demonstrated incidence and still must not be wired into `phoenix_fuse` as recovered behaviour.

- `CLM-PIPELINE-001` / `CLM-CORRECTION-001` HighlightRestore Bayer-kernel addendum III — horizontal reductions and the global floating-point environment (clean-room, Phoenix side, 2026-08-05):

  `GAP-9B-3d.7` is **closed**, settled entirely from the disassembly and the constant pool of `libcp.dylib`. **No parity-corpus frame was rendered, scored or consulted for any statement in this addendum.** The full derivation is `_parity_run/STAGE9B_HRB_KERNEL_MATH.md` §8, mirrored into `phoenix/PARITY_BLOCKER_MAP.md` and the `highlight_restore_rgba.h` header block.

  Every horizontal sum in the phase kernel is the four-instruction idiom `movaps Z,V ; shufpd Z,Z,0x1 ; addps Z,V ; movaps W,Z ; shufps W,W,0xb1 ; addps W,Z`, with `dst == src` on both shuffles. Tracing the lanes gives the pairwise tree `hsum(V) = fadd( fadd(v1,v3), fadd(v0,v2) )`, with the odd-lane pair as the outer add's **left** operand. All four result lanes come out bitwise identical for non-NaN inputs, so the `shufps 0x0` broadcast that normally follows is lane-agnostic. Addendum I above stated this association as `((v0+v2)+(v1+v3))`, which is the same value; this addendum pins the operand order that the value alone does not determine.

  The census is exact rather than approximate. `shufps ..., 0xb1` occurs **24** times in `0x30b9f0 .. 0x30dcc0` and every occurrence is an hsum tail, so the kernel performs exactly 24 horizontal sums. `shufpd ..., 0x1` occurs **26** times: 20 head a standalone hsum, **4 are shared with a horizontal max**, and 2 are loop B's site-2 lane-2 selectors already recorded in §7.4. An earlier working note that called these "24 identical hsum sites" was correct in count and wrong in structure, and is superseded here.

  Lane 3 is not uniformly zero. Four of the 24 sums are genuine 4-wide reductions — the diagonal-chroma weight and product sums at tails `0x30c2f0`, `0x30c305`, `0x30da5b`, `0x30da70`, two in each loop. Sixteen carry `+0.0` blended in from an `xorps`-zeroed register (`xmm14` in loop A, `xmm8` inside cascade instantiations 2 and 3, `xmm15` in loop B). The remaining **four carry `-0.0f`** at tails `0x30c469`, `0x30cc39`, `0x30d55a`, `0x30dbc9`, because there the blend source is the cascade accumulator, whose lane 3 is exactly `-0.9f` after `addps acc, {-0.9f x4}` at `0x5f3e40`, multiplied by lane 3 of `[rbp-0xf0]` which is `+0.0f`. The sign is invisible except when every lane is zero, but it must be reproduced literally rather than replaced by a zero. That this table partitions four-per-cascade is an independent confirmation of addendum II's result that the cascade is one body instantiated four times.

  The four shared heads at `0x30c3fb`, `0x30cbcf`, `0x30d4f0` and `0x30db5f` feed a horizontal **max**: `maxss( maxps(V, shufpd(V,0x1)), movshdup(...) )` with lane 3 forced to `+0.0`, i.e. `max(v0, v1, v2, 0.0f)`. x86 `MAXPS` and `MAXSS` return the *source* operand on ties, when either operand is a NaN, and for `max(+0.0, -0.0)`; a port must write `(a > b) ? a : b` with the operands in the same order, and must not use `std::max` or `fmaxf`.

  `[rbp-0xf0]` is identified as the unit vector `(v - a) / |v - a|` with lane 3 forced to `+0.0`, built at `0x30bb42 .. 0x30bba6` from `sqrtss` followed by `divss` — both correctly rounded, so unlike the `rcpss`/`rcpps`/`rsqrtps` sites it needs no `GAP-9B-3d.6` emulation — with the squared length accumulated in the order `fadd(dz*dz, fadd(dy*dy, dx*dx))`.

  Two global floating-point facts are settled deterministically by sweeping all **1,333,499** disassembled instructions of `libcp.dylib`: the counts of `ldmxcsr`, `stmxcsr`, `vfmadd*`, `vfmsub*`, `vfnmadd*`, `ymm` and `zmm` are **all zero**. So the library contains no fused multiply-add anywhere — every `a*b + c` in the kernel is a separately-rounded multiply then add, and a C port must be built with contraction disabled (`-ffp-contract=off`), since a single contracted FMA changes the low bit. And the library never reads or writes MXCSR, so it never alters rounding mode, FTZ or DAZ. The image is also pure SSE: there is no VEX-encoded variant of any of these instructions anywhere in it, which removes a whole class of denormal- and zero-sign-behaviour ambiguity. For contrast, the library does contain `roundss` x115, `roundps` x8, `divps` x157 and `rsqrtss` x2 — none of them inside the phase kernel.

  Two new, narrow gaps are opened rather than glossed. `GAP-9B-3d.7a`: hsum lane equality is bitwise only for non-NaN inputs, since `ADDPS` returns the *destination* operand's NaN and the destination register differs between lane 0 and lane 2; no kernel path is known to feed a NaN there. `GAP-9B-3d.7b`: proving libcp never *changes* the FP environment does not prove what that environment *is* — the Lumen application could set FTZ/DAZ or a non-default rounding mode before calling in, so the working assumption of round-to-nearest-even with FTZ and DAZ off is the macOS x86-64 default, **inherited and not proved from this image**. Settling it requires reading MXCSR under lldb at entry to `0x30b9f0`, which is a capture task, not a static one.

  Still open and explicitly not claimed: `GAP-9B-3d.3` (what statistic buffer B carries), `GAP-9B-3d.4` (the object at `ctx + 0x198`), **`GAP-9B-3d.6`** (exact unrefined `rcpss`/`rcpps`/`rsqrtps` emulation — now the single remaining parity-critical blocker in this kernel; close it by wiring in the integer-only bit formula in `bundle_runtime_x86_rcpss_rcpps_exact_emulation.md`, verified over 6,242,316 cases, and do not re-derive it), `GAP-9B-3d.7a`, `GAP-9B-3d.7b`, `GAP-9B-3c`, and `GAP-9B-3f`. No claim status changes. The `0x3440b0` vec4 kernel still has no demonstrated incidence and still must not be wired into `phoenix_fuse` as recovered behaviour.

- `CLM-PIPELINE-001` / `CLM-CORRECTION-001` HighlightRestore Bayer-kernel addendum IV — `GAP-9B-3d.6` **CLOSED**, Stage 9b is bit-exact (clean-room, Phoenix side, 2026-08-05):

  `GAP-9B-3d.6` — exact emulation of the unrefined `rcpss` / `rcpps` / `rsqrtps` seeds — is **closed**, and with it the last unproven arithmetic in the phase kernel. `lt::RestoreHighlightsBayer` now reproduces Lumen **bit-for-bit**: all six ground-truth src/dst tile pairs captured under lldb on L16_03041 come back `exact = 100.0000 %`, `maxdiff = 0 DN`, up from `96.65 – 99.76 %` with `maxdiff = 1`. **No parity-corpus frame was rendered, scored or consulted at any point.** Every change was derived from the disassembly of `libcp.dylib`, from constants read out of the image, or from an exhaustive hardware sweep of an instruction's own semantics; the six captured tiles are Lumen intermediates and serve as proof under the evidence hierarchy, not as end-to-end correlation. Full derivation: `_parity_run/STAGE9B_HRB_KERNEL_MATH.md` §9–§11.

  **The `rcpss`/`rcpps` half** was closed by reuse, as directed: `phoenix/engine/common/x86_rcp.h`, the integer bit formula already carried by `bundle_runtime_x86_rcpss_rcpps_exact_emulation.md`, wired into `engine/depth/highlight_restore.cpp` at all 20 sites (2 `rcpps`, 18 `rcpss`). It was **not** re-derived.

  **The `rsqrtps` half had no prior emulation anywhere in the project** and is newly derived here. Its geometry was measured rather than borrowed from the `rcpss` result, and the two differ — borrowing would have been wrong. For each of the 254 finite exponents, ORing together every fraction at which the hardware result changes, across all `2^23` fractions, gives bit 13 uniformly. The `rsqrt` seed is therefore a **1024-entry table indexed by `fraction >> 13`, selected by the exponent's parity** (2048 entries: same table size as `rcpss`, different index width, plus a parity split that `rcpss` does not have, because an odd binary exponent must fold a factor of two into the radicand). Confirmed independently: the result mantissa is identical for exponents `e` and `e + 2` at every bin, and the low **11** output fraction bits are always clear, leaving a 12-bit significand. With `j = fraction >> 13`, `d = 2049 + 2j` and `p = 1 - (exponent & 1)`, the value is `Q = round( sqrt( 2^(37-p) / d ) )` in `[4096, 8192]`, encoded as `((126 - s) << 23) | ((Q - 4096) << 11)` with `s = (exponent - 127 - p)/2` (the numerator is always even, so the halving is exact). `d` is odd and greater than one, so `2^(39-p)/d` is never an exact `(2Q+1)^2` and **the rounding has no ties to break** — no tie-break rule had to be guessed. The shipped C++ (`phoenix/engine/common/x86_rsqrt.h`) finds `Q` by binary search on `(2Q+1)^2 * d > 2^(39-p)`: pure 64-bit integer, `constexpr`, no `libm`.

  **Verification is exhaustive, not sampled.** `tools/validation/dump_x86_rsqrtss.c` (sibling of `dump_x86_rcpss.c`) has a `structure` pass that re-derives the geometry above from hardware with no assumptions, and a `selftest` pass that compares the formula against hardware `RSQRTSS` for **all 4,294,967,296 input bit patterns** and against `RSQRTPS` lane 0 on a stride-1021 sweep of the same space. Result: **0 mismatches**, both passes. The exact header that ships was itself re-verified over the full `2^32` space, so the proof covers the shipped code rather than a model of it. Driver: `tools/validation/verify_x86_rsqrt_emulation.sh`. Specials confirmed: `+0`/`+subnormal -> +inf`, `-0`/`-subnormal -> -inf`, `+inf -> +0`, `-inf -> 0xffc00000`, any negative normal `-> 0xffc00000`, NaN `-> bits | 0x00400000`.

  **Ten further divergences were found by transcribing the prologue** (`0x30ba48 .. 0x30bc9a`) rather than assuming it, and they account for the improvement from `96.6 – 99.8 %` to `99.6 – 99.98 %` that preceded the `rsqrt` fix. The kernel maintains `inv1 = 1/c1` (the port had no such term); `ws = 1/(white - black)` computed at runtime (the port hardcoded `1/981`); `norm[1] = inv1 * ws` and `k085[1] = inv1 * 0.85f` (the port assumed `c1 == 1` and used the bare values); `ratio_r = inv0 * c1` and `ratio_b = inv2 * c1` feeding the Q10 truncations `q_red` and `q_blue`; `denorm[i] = (white - black) * c[i]` (the port hardcoded `981.0f`); and — the subtle one — `c0_c1 = inv1 / inv0` at `0x30bbb2` and `c2_c1 = inv1 / inv2` at `0x30bbc3`, i.e. the kernel divides the **reciprocals**, which is algebraically equal to `c0/c1` and **not bitwise equal**, since each reciprocal is rounded before the quotient is taken. The unit vector `[rbp-0xf0]` was also rebuilt from its instructions: its middle component is `1.0f - 0.9f = 0.10000002f`, where the port had hardcoded the literal `0.1f`.

  **A methodological point worth recording.** Every `c1`-dependent correction above is a bitwise no-op when `c1 == 1.0f`, because `x * 1.0f == x` and `1.0f / 1.0f == 1.0f` are exact in IEEE-754, and all six captured tiles carry `c1 == 1`. Those defects were therefore **undiscoverable by scoring against captured output at any effort level**, and equally could not have regressed it; they only make the port correct for a camera where `c1 != 1`. This is the concrete case for reading the binary rather than fitting the output.

  **Lane 3 of `p` is resolved rather than assumed.** Addendum III left `num`'s lane-3 sign unresolved. `xorps xmm14, xmm14` at `0x30bf36`, `[rbp-0x100] = broadcast(-black)` (built by `xorps` against `0x5a88e0 = -0.0f x4` at `0x30bc67`), and `[rbp-0xe0]` lane 3 `= +0.0f` (`blendps xmm5, xmm1, 0xe` at `0x30babb`) together give `p[3] = (+0.0f + (-black)) * +0.0f = -0.0f` for any positive black level. So `num`'s lane-3 addend is `0.0f * p[3] = -0.0f`, and `dot`'s is `d3 * kdir[3]` with `d3 = -0.9f` and `kdir[3] = +0.0f`. Under round-to-nearest-even `fadd(x, -0.0f) == x` for every `x`, whereas `fadd(x, +0.0f) == x` for every `x` except `x == -0.0f`; carrying the true lane-3 value explicitly is exact and simpler than case-analysing it.

  **Two reduction findings that addendum III's work list did not anticipate.** `wsum`'s **4-tap** reduction at `0x30c2a0 .. 0x30c322` is a genuine 4-wide pairwise hsum — a real divergence from a left-to-right accumulation loop — while its **2-tap** reduction at `0x30ca10 .. 0x30cb00` is fully scalar (`addss num1, num0`), which `hsum4(a, b, +0.0f, +0.0f)` reproduces exactly. These were found by checking rather than by extrapolating addendum III's idiom; had only the listed items been applied, the 4-tap divergence would have survived. Separately, the `maxss` operand order is **mirrored within one basic block**: `maxss xmm6(= +0.0f), xmm4(= dot)` at `0x30c473` and `0x30c486` lets the *source* win, while `maxss xmm0(= S - 2), mem(= +0.0f)` at `0x30c4ba` lets the *destination* win. Writing the same C expression at both sites is wrong.

  **A prior claim is corrected, not deleted.** `phoenix/engine/depth/highlight_restore.h` previously asserted that bit-exactness was *unattainable*: "the capture ran under Rosetta 2, whose `rcpps` is itself an emulation, so ±1 DN is below the original binary's own cross-host determinism floor." That was wrong on both counts. Rosetta 2 reproduces Intel's architectural seed tables exactly — which is why the `rcpss` formula originally derived under Rosetta validates unchanged against native Intel silicon — and the ±1 DN was not a determinism floor but two specific defects in the port. The header now carries the correction inline alongside the superseded text.

  **Still open and explicitly not claimed:** `GAP-9B-3d.3` (what statistic buffer B carries), `GAP-9B-3d.4` (the object at `ctx + 0x198`), `GAP-9B-3d.7a` (hsum lane equality is bitwise only for non-NaN inputs), `GAP-9B-3d.7b` (libcp never *changes* the FP environment, which does not prove what it *is*; needs an MXCSR read under lldb at entry to `0x30b9f0`), `GAP-9B-3c`, and `GAP-9B-3f`. No claim status changes. `engine/depth/highlight_restore_rgba.cpp` — the vec4 sibling `lt::RestoreHighlights`, kernel `0x315630` — still carries the old `trunc12` placeholders for both seeds; it has no demonstrated incidence, is not wired into `phoenix_fuse`, and **must not be** on the strength of this addendum.
